   - `keep:` takes a ONE-LINE reason. Use it for the expensive and the

   - `delete:` takes `redownloadable` (and a `source:` naming the repo id, URL

A CHECKER RUNS WHEN YOU SUBMIT. If anything heavy has no decision it fails

WHAT NEEDS NO DECISION — do not write entries for these:
- text and code files, at ANY size (source, JSON, CSV, YAML, logs, markdown);
- anything under the auto-keep floor (10 MB), whatever it holds.
Only large binaries and cache directories (`hf_cache/`, `.venv/`,

NEVER mark your results, figures, papers, code, logs or anything a later step

WHAT A `keep` BUYS YOU. Anything you do not mark `delete` stays exactly where
you wrote it, on this run's storage volume, at the path it already has — it is
not moved, renamed or copied. A later round reads it there, by that absolute
workspace path, so a checkpoint you keep is a checkpoint the next round can
load instead of retraining. It is also the ONLY copy: the publish step pushes
your cwd to GitHub but skips every file of 100 MB or
more, so trained weights and large binary artifacts never leave the volume.
Name each kept artifact in your results and your `README.md` by its path
RELATIVE to your cwd, and say it stays on the run's volume rather than in the
published repository. Never write an absolute server path into a file that is
published: a reader's machine has none of them.

<artifact_info>
id: art_WiY4wqHLb9Gy
type: experiment
title: Show a solution, ban it, see what replaces it
summary: >-
  Exposure-ban experiment (Step 1b) on Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking off, vLLM 0.11.0, revision b968826d) over the 12 frozen tasks
  of the exp11 algorithm-choice bench (sha256-verified copy in bench_copy/; labeller re-check reproduces every gate.json value,
  references 100%). For each of 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y (19 priority Y whose frozen associate differs from the
  default), the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks COPY 'Write a correct solution.', NAMED
  '... that does not use {Y}.' (= template W5) or UNNAMED '... uses a different algorithm from the one above.' (N = 50 per
  Y and arm, seeds shared across arms). Four forecast families are committed in a sha256 hash chain before any ban sample:
  LADDER (default), ASSOCIATE (modal frozen 'standard alternative to Y'), PROXIMITY (nearest survivor by Zhang-Shasha distance
  on identifier-normalised ASTs; token-distance sensitivity) and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT (non-Y landing under an iterated persistent
  signature veto of Y's distinctive token n-gram under the copy-twin prompt, implemented as a vLLM V1 per-request logits processor;
  GPU smoke tests: 0/20 vetoed n-grams vs 20/20 in the copy twin, inert veto 20/20 identical, twin divergence exactly at the
  vetoed position 20/20). Outputs: data/samples.jsonl (one row per program, gated bench labeller: runtime + AST + blind judge),
  results/c1_events.jsonl (one row per (Y, NAMED/UNNAMED) event in the frozen schema with forecasts, top-1s, log scores, gate),
  results/c1_summary.json (frozen advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests with task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch
  verdict, sensitivities, C1-exposure, placebos), results/other_reports.json (copy rates, snap-back to default, named-vs-unnamed
  agreement, disguise, heterogeneity, veto diagnostics, template arm), predictions/ (committed forecasts and prompts), commitments.log
  (COMMIT #0/#1/#2 hash chain), audit/rederive.py (independent re-derivation + placebos), figures/, and method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out:
  datasets exposure_events and exposure_samples). Note: this summary is interim and is rewritten with the final numbers when
  the analysis finishes.
workspace_path: >-
  /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_aRfWvcSWjssb/3_invention_loop/iter_3/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_14

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-3/experiment-14/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

int(sha256_text(key)[:8], 16)\n\n\ndef read_jsonl(p: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    if not Path(p).exists():\n        return []\n    out = []\n    with Path(p).open() as f:\n        for line in f:\n            line = line.strip()\n            if line:\n                out.append(json.loads(line))\n    return out\n\n\n_write_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n\ndef append_jsonl(p: Path, rows: list[dict]) ->      return o.item()\n    if isinstance(o, (set, tuple)):\n        return list(o)\n    raise TypeError(f\"not JSON serialisable: {type(o)}\")\n\n\ndef read_json(p: Path):\n    return 1.959964) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    if n == 0:\n        return (float(\"nan\"), float(\"nan\"))\n    p = k / n\n    d = 1 + z * z / n\n    c = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / d\n    h = z * math.sqrt(p * path = RESULTS / \"timings.json\"\n\n    def __init__(self, stage: str, gpu: bool = False):\n        self.stage, self.gpu = stage, gpu\n\n    def __enter__(self):\n        self.t0 = time.time()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, *exc):\n        dt = time.time() - self.t0\n        RESULTS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n        tm = read_json(self.path) if self.path.exists() else {}\n        e = tm.setdefault(self.stage, {\"wall_s\": 0.0, \"gpu_s\": 0.0, \"runs\": 0})\n        e[\"wall_s\"] += dt\n        e[\"gpu_s\"] += dt if self.gpu else 0.0\n        e[\"runs\"] += 1\n        e[\"last_end_utc\"] = time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime())\n        write_json(self.path, tm)\n        logger.info(f\"[timing] {self.stage}: {dt:.1f}s ({'GPU' if self.gpu else 'CPU'})\")\n        return False\n\n\ndef load_tokenizer():\n    from transformers import AutoTokenizer\n    return AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_ID, revision=MODEL_REV)\n\n\ndef deviation(text: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Record a declared deviation from the plan (results/deviations.jsonl), deduplicated by text.\"\"\"\n    p = RESULTS / \"deviations.jsonl\"\n    RESULTS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n    have = {r[\"text\"] for r in read_jsonl(p)}\n    if text not in have:\n        append_jsonl(p, [{\"ts\": time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime()), \"text\": text}])\n        logger.warning(f\"DEVIATION: {text}\")\n=====VETO=====\n\"\"\"Persistent n-gram veto as a vLLM V1 per-request logits processor (AdapterLogitsProcessor, vllm==0.11.0).\n\nA request opts in with SamplingParams(extra_args={\"veto\": [[t1, ..., tn], ...]}).  At every decoding step the last\ntoken of each vetoed n-gram gets logit -inf whenever the preceding n-1 GENERATED tokens equal the n-gram's prefix\n(a 1-gram is banned at every step).  Only generated ids are inspected, never the prompt, so a vetoed id sequence\ncan never occur inside the generated ids.  Requests without the key get no processor (the batch is untouched).\nLoaded by the engine-core process through its FQCN \"exposure.veto:NgramVeto\" (the workspace is on PYTHONPATH).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nimport torch\nfrom vllm import SamplingParams\nfrom vllm.v1.sample.logits_processor import AdapterLogitsProcessor\n\n\nclass RequestVeto:\n    \"\"\"Per-request callable f(output_ids, logits_row) -> logits_row (modified in place).\"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"table\", \"lens\", \"_dev\")\n\n    def __init__(self, ngrams: list[list[int]]):\n        table: dict[tuple[int, ...], set[int]] = {}\n        for g in ngrams:\n            g = tuple(int(x) for x in g)\n            if g:\n                table.setdefault(g[:-1], set()).add(g[-1])\n        self.table = {k: torch.tensor(sorted(v), dtype=torch.long) for k, v in table.items()}\n        self.lens = sorted({len(k) for k in table})\n        self._dev = None\n\n    def __call__(self, output_ids: list[int], logits: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n        if self._dev is None or self._dev != logits.device:\n            self.table = {k: v.to(logits.device) for k, v in self.table.items()}\n            self._dev = logits.device\n        n_out = len(output_ids)\n        for length in self.lens:\n            if length > n_out:\n                break\n            key = tuple(output_ids[n_out - length:]) if length else ()\n            banned = self.table.get(key)\n            if banned is not None:\n                logits[banned] = float(\"-inf\")\n        return logits\n\n\nclass NgramVeto(AdapterLogitsProcessor):\n    @classmethod\n    def validate_params(cls, params: SamplingParams):\n        ea = params.extra_args or {}\n        v = ea.get(\"veto\")\n        if v is not None and not (isinstance(v, list) and all(isinstance(g, list) and g for g in v)):\n            raise ValueError(\"extra_args['veto'] must be a list of non-empty token-id lists\")\n\n    def is_argmax_invariant(self) -> bool:\n        return False\n\n    def new_req_logits_processor(self, params: SamplingParams) -> Optional[RequestVeto]:\n        ea = params.extra_args or {}\n        v = ea.get(\"veto\")\n        if not v:\n            return None\n        return RequestVeto(v)\n=====NGRAM=====\n\"\"\"Token n-gram utilities: per-program n-gram sets, the frozen distinctive-n-gram rule, occurrence search.\n\nFrozen rule (plan step 0.7 / 2.4):\n  candidates   = n-grams (n = 1..6) whose presence (share of reference programs containing them) >= 0.8,\n                 minus n-grams already in the veto mask;\n  collateral   = max over collateral groups of the share of the group's programs containing the n-gram;\n  order        = (collateral asc, n asc, presence desc, token ids asc);\n  shown rule   = the first candidate contained in >= min(5, |P_Y|) shown-pool programs, else the candidate with the\n                 largest shown count (round 1 only; later rounds have no shown constraint);\n  relaxed rule = (later rounds only) if no candidate reaches 0.8, the highest-presence n-gram with collateral < 0.5.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom collections import Counter\n\n\ndef ngram_set(ids: list[int], nmax: int = 6) -> set[tuple[int, ...]]:\n    out: set[tuple[int, ...]] = set()\n    n_ids = len(ids)\n    for n in range(1, nmax + 1):\n        for i in range(n_ids - n + 1):\n            out.add(tuple(ids[i:i + n]))\n    return out\n\n\ndef contains(ids: list[int], g: tuple[int, ...] | list[int]) -> bool:\n    g = tuple(g)\n    n = len(g)\n    if n == 0 or n > len(ids):\n        return False\n    first = g[0]\n    for i in range(len(ids) - n + 1):\n        if ids[i] == first and tuple(ids[i:i + n]) == g:\n            return True\n    return False\n\n\ndef occurrences(ids: list[int], g: tuple[int, ...] | list[int]) -> list[int]:\n    g = tuple(g)\n    n = len(g)\n    return [i for i in range(len(ids) - n + 1) if tuple(ids[i:i + n]) == g] if n else []\n\n\ndef group_shares(groups: dict[str, list[set]]) -> dict[str, tuple[Counter, int]]:\n    \"\"\"Per group: Counter over n-grams of the member sets and the group size.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    for name, sets in groups.items():\n        c: Counter = Counter()\n        for s in sets:\n            c.update(s)\n        out[name] = (c, len(sets))\n    return out\n\n\ndef collateral_of(g: tuple[int, ...], shares: dict[str, tuple[Counter, int]]) -> tuple[float, dict[str, float]]:\n    by = {name: (c[g] / n if n else 0.0) for name, (c, n) in shares.items()}\n    return (max(by.values()) if by else 0.0), by\n\n\ndef select_distinctive(ref_sets: list[set], groups: dict[str, list[set]], shown_sets: list[set] | None = None,\n                       presence_min: float = 0.8, exclude: set | None = None, allow_relaxed: bool = False,\n                       relaxed_collateral_max: float = 0.5, coveto_share: float = 0.5,\n                       groups_correct: dict[str, list[set]] | None = None) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"Apply the frozen distinctive-n-gram rule.  Returns None when there is no reference program.\"\"\"\n    exclude = exclude or set()\n    n_ref = len(ref_sets)\n    if n_ref == 0:\n        return None\n    pres: Counter = Counter()\n    for s in ref_sets:\n        pres.update(s)\n    shares = group_shares(groups)\n    cand = [g for g, c in pres.items() if c / n_ref >= presence_min and g not in exclude]\n    relaxed = False\n    if not cand:\n        if not allow_relaxed:\n            return {\"ids\": None, \"n_candidates\": 0, \"relaxed\": False, \"reason\": \"no candidate reaches presence\"}\n        pool = [g for g in pres if g not in exclude]\n        scored = [(g, pres[g] / n_ref, collateral_of(g, shares)[0]) for g in pool]\n        scored = [x for x in scored if x[2] < relaxed_collateral_max]\n        if not scored:\n            return {\"ids\": None, \"n_candidates\": 0, \"relaxed\": True, \"reason\": \"no n-gram with collateral < 0.5\"}\n        scored.sort(key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[2], len(x[0]), x[0]))\n        cand = [scored[0][0]]\n        relaxed = True\n    keyed = []\n    for g in cand:\n        col, _ = collateral_of(g, shares)\n        keyed.append(((col, len(g), -pres[g] / n_ref, g), g))\n    keyed.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])\n    ordered = [g for _, g in keyed]\n    chosen, shown_count = None, None\n    if shown_sets is not None:\n        need = min(5, len(shown_sets))\n        counts = {g: sum(1 for s in shown_sets if g in s) for g in ordered}\n        for g in ordered:\n            if counts[g] >= need:\n                chosen = g\n                break\n        if chosen is None:  # the candidate with the largest shown count (first in rule order among ties)\n            best = max(counts.values()) if counts else 0\n            chosen = next(g for g in ordered if counts[g] == best)\n        shown_count = counts[chosen]\n    else:\n        chosen = ordered[0]\n    col, by = collateral_of(chosen, shares)\n    out = {\"ids\": list(chosen), \"n\": len(chosen), \"presence\": pres[chosen] / n_ref, \"collateral\": col,\n           \"collateral_by_group\": by, \"coveto_groups\": sorted(k for k, v in by.items() if v >= coveto_share),\n           \"n_candidates\": len(cand), \"relaxed\": relaxed, \"shown_count\": shown_count, \"n_ref\": n_ref,\n           \"rank_in_rule_order\": ordered.index(chosen)}\n    if groups_correct is not None:\n        _, byc = collateral_of(chosen, group_shares(groups_correct))\n        out[\"collateral_by_group_correct_only\"] = byc\n    return out\n=====ASTDIST=====\n\"\"\"Identifier-normalised Python ASTs and Zhang-Shasha tree edit distance (zss.simple_distance, unit costs).\n\nNormalisation (plan step 0.9): docstrings dropped; user-defined names, arguments, functions and assigned attributes\nrenamed v0, v1, ... in order of first appearance (pre-order, source-field order); builtins, imported module names,\ntheir attributes and every other attribute / keyword name (API names such as heapq.heappush, bisect_left, Counter,\nappend) kept verbatim; the entry point `solve` kept; constant values replaced by their type tag; expression-context\nnodes (Load/Store/Del) dropped.  Node label = node type, plus the operator type(s), plus the (kept or normalised) name.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport ast\nimport builtins\n\nimport zss\n\n_BUILTINS = set(dir(builtins))\n_SKIP = (ast.expr_context,)\n\n\ndef _strip_docstrings(tree: ast.AST) -> None:\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.Module, ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)) and node.body:\n            f = node.body[0]\n            if isinstance(f, ast.Expr) and isinstance(f.value, ast.Constant) and isinstance(f.value.value, str):\n                node.body = node.body[1:] or [ast.Pass()]\n\n\ndef _imported(tree: ast.AST) -> set[str]:\n    out = set()\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):\n            for a in node.names:\n                out.add((a.asname or a.name).split(\".\")[0])\n        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):\n            for a in node.names:\n                out.add(a.asname or a.name)\n            if node.module:\n                out.add(node.module.split(\".\")[0])\n    return out\n\n\ndef _user_attrs(tree: ast.AST) -> set[str]:\n    return {n.attr for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n, ast.Attribute) and isinstance(n.ctx, ast.Store)}\n\n\nclass _Normaliser:\n    def __init__(self, tree: ast.AST):\n        self.kept = _BUILTINS | _imported(tree) | {\"solve\"}\n        self.user_attrs = _user_attrs(tree)\n        self.names: dict[str, str] = {}\n\n    def name(self, s: str) -> str:\n        if s in self.kept:\n            return s\n        if s not in self.names:\n            self.names[s] = f\"v{len(self.names)}\"\n        return self.names[s]\n\n    def label(self, node: ast.AST) -> str:\n        t = type(node).__name__\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.BoolOp, ast.AugAssign)):\n            return f\"{t}:{type(node.op).__name__}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Compare):\n            return f\"{t}:{','.join(type(o).__name__ for o in node.ops)}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Name):\n            return f\"{t}:{self.name(node.id)}\"\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):\n            return f\"{t}:{self.name(node.name)}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.arg):\n            return f\"{t}:{self.name(node.arg)}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Attribute):\n            return f\"{t}:{self.name(node.attr) if node.attr in self.user_attrs else node.attr}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):\n            return f\"{t}:{type(node.value).__name__}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.keyword):\n            return f\"{t}:{node.arg}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.alias):\n            return f\"{t}:{node.name}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):\n            return f\"{t}:{node.module}\"\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.Global, ast.Nonlocal)):\n            return f\"{t}:{','.join(self.name(n) for n in node.names)}\"\n        if isinstance(node, ast.ExceptHandler) and node.name:\n            return f\"{t}:{self.name(node.name)}\"\n        return t\n\n    def build(self, node: ast.AST) -> zss.Node:\n        z = zss.Node(self.label(node))\n        for ch in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):\n            if isinstance(ch, _SKIP):\n                continue\n            z.addkid(self.build(ch))\n        return z\n\n\ndef normalised_tree(code: str) -> zss.Node | None:\n    try:\n        tree = ast.parse(code)\n    except (SyntaxError, ValueError):\n        return None\n    _strip_docstrings(tree)\n    return _Normaliser(tree).build(tree)\n\n\ndef tree_size(z: zss.Node) -> int:\n    return 1 + sum(tree_size(c) for c in z.children)\n\n\ndef distance(a: zss.Node, b: zss.Node) -> float:\n    return float(zss.simple_distance(a, b))\n\n\ndef pair_distance(args: tuple[str, str, str]) -> tuple[str, float | None]:\n    \"\"\"Process-pool worker: (key, code_a, code_b) -> (key, Zhang-Shasha distance or None if unparsable).\"\"\"\n    key, code_a, code_b = args\n    ta, tb = normalised_tree(code_a), normalised_tree(code_b)\n    if ta is None or tb is None:\n        return key, None\n    return key, distance(ta, tb)", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

{"stdout": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Independent re-derivation of every c1_events row and the headline numbers (plan T5 / 4.7).\n\nReads only data/samples.jsonl, the committed predictions/ files and commitments.log.  Imports nothing from analysis/\nor exposure/: smoothing, log scores, top-1, gate, advance rule, an exact Wilcoxon signed-rank test (dynamic\nprogramming over rank sums), Holm and the branch are re-implemented here.  Then compares against results/c1_events.jsonl\nand results/c1_summary.json, and runs placebo checks that must FAIL to find an effect:\n  (1) sign-flip placebo: random signs on the per-event log-score differences -> Holm-significant rarely (<= ~5%);\n  (2) constant-forecast placebo: every family forecasts Uniform(S) -> no family wins any event;\n  (3) within-task rotation of the Y-specific forecasts (SUBTRACT, ASSOCIATE, PROXIMITY) -> reported.\nWrites results/rederive.json and exits non-zero on any mismatch.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nimport math\nimport random\nimport sys\nfrom collections import Counter, defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nTOL = 1e-9\n\n\ndef jl(p: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    return [json.loads(x) for x in p.read_text().splitlines() if x.strip()]\n\n\ndef jread(p: Path):\n    return json.loads(p.read_text())\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- re-implementations\ndef smoothed(f, surv):\n    k = len(surv)\n    g = {}\n    if f:\n        g = {a: float(b) for a, b in f.items() if (a in surv or a == \"other\") and b > 0}\n    tot = sum(g.values())\n    g = {a: b / tot for a, b in g.items()} if tot > 0 else {a: 1.0 / k for a in surv}\n    p = {a: 0.98 * (0.95 * g.get(a, 0.0) + 0.05 / k) for a in surv}\n    p[\"other\"] = 0.98 * 0.95 * g.get(\"other\", 0.0) + 0.02\n    return p, g\n\n\ndef argmax_set(d):\n    if not d:\n        return []\n    m = max(d.values())\n    return sorted(a for a, b in d.items() if abs(b - m) < 1e-12)\n\n\ndef exact_wilcoxon_p(d):\n    \"\"\"Two-sided exact signed-rank p (zeros dropped, average ranks for ties, exact over integerised half-ranks).\"\"\"\n    x = [v for v in d if v != 0]\n    n = len(x)\n    if n == 0:\n        return 1.0\n    a = sorted(range(n), key=lambda i: abs(x[i]))\n    ranks = [0.0] * n\n    i = 0\n    while i < n:\n        j = i\n        while j + 1 < n and abs(x[a[j + 1]]) == abs(x[a[i]]):\n            j += 1\n        for k in range(i, j + 1):\n            ranks[a[k]] = (i + j) / 2 + 1\n        i = j + 1\n    r2 = [int(round(2 * r)) for r in ranks]  # half-ranks are integers\n    wplus = sum(r for r, v in zip(r2, x) if v > 0)\n    tot = sum(r2)\n    if n > 25:\n        mu = sum(ranks) / 2\n        var = sum(r * r for r in ranks) / 4\n        z = (wplus / 2 - mu) / math.sqrt(var)\n        return math.erfc(abs(z) / math.sqrt(2))\n    counts = {0: 1}\n    for r in r2:\n        nxt = defaultdict(int)\n        for s, c in counts.items():\n            nxt[s] += c\n            nxt[s + r] += c\n        counts = nxt\n    total = 2 ** n\n    lo = sum(c for s, c in counts.items() if s <= min(wplus, tot - wplus))\n    return min(1.0, 2 * lo / total)\n\n\ndef holm(ps):\n    m = len(ps)\n    order = sorted(range(m), key=lambda i: ps[i])\n    out, run = [0.0] * m, 0.0\n    for rank, i in enumerate(order):\n        run = max(run, min(1.0, (m - rank) * ps[i]))\n        out[i] = run\n    return out\n\n\nFAM = [\"SUBTRACT\", \"LADDER\", \"ASSOCIATE\", \"PROXIMITY\"]\nPAIRS = [(\"SUBTRACT\", \"ASSOCIATE\"), (\"SUBTRACT\", \"PROXIMITY\"), (\"LADDER\", \"ASSOCIATE\"), (\"LADDER\", \"PROXIMITY\")]\n\n\ndef score(land, surv, forecasts):\n    n = sum(land.values())\n    ls, t1 = {}, {}\n    for f in FAM:\n        p, g = smoothed(forecasts[f], surv)\n        ls[f] = sum(c * math.log(p[lab]) for lab, c in land.items()) / n if n else float(\"nan\")\n        t1[f] = argmax_set(g)\n    dis = len({tuple(v) for v in t1.values()}) > 1\n    return ls, t1, dis\n\n\ndef wins_of(evs):\n    w = Counter()\n    for e in evs:\n        for f in FAM:\n            if e[\"ls\"][f] - max(e[\"ls\"][g] for g in FAM if g != f) >= 0.10:\n                w[f] += 1\n    return {f: w[f] for f in FAM}\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    samples = jl(ROOT / \"data\" / \"samples.jsonl\")\n    pred = ROOT / \"predictions\"\n    elig = jread(pred / \"eligibility.json\")\n    asc, lad = jread(pred / \"associate.json\"), jread(pred / \"ladder.json\")\n    prox, sub = jread(pred / \"proximity.json\"), jread(pred / \"subtract_in_context.json\")\n    shown = Counter(r[\"key\"] for r in jl(pred / \"shown_programs.jsonl\"))\n    rows = defaultdict(list)\n    for r in samples:\n        if r[\"Y\"]:\n            rows[(r[\"task\"], r[\"Y\"], r[\"arm\"])].append(r)\n    mine = {}\n    for key in shown:\n        t, y = key.split(\":\")\n        surv = [m for m in elig[\"tasks\"][t][\"modes\"] if m != y]\n        fc = {\"SUBTRACT\": sub[key][\"distribution\"], \"LADDER\": lad[key], \"ASSOCIATE\": asc[key],\n              \"PROXIMITY\": prox[key][\"distribution\"]}\n        for arm in (\"named\", \"unnamed\"):\n            rs = rows.get((t, y, arm), [])\n            if not rs:\n                continue\n            att = [r[\"label\"] for r in rs if r[\"label\"] != \"invalid\"]\n            ny = att.count(y)\n            non = [lab if lab in surv else \"other\" for lab in att if lab != y]\n            land = Counter(non)\n            avoid = 1 - ny / len(att) if att else float(\"nan\")\n            ls, t1, dis = score(land, surv, fc)\n            mine[(t, y, arm)] = {\"avoidance\": avoid, \"n_nonY\": len(non), \"gate\": (avoid >= 0.5 and len(non) >= 20),\n                                 \"landing\": dict(land), \"ls\": ls, \"t1\": t1, \"dis\": dis, \"task\": t, \"Y\": y, \"surv\": surv,\n                                 \"fc\": fc}\n    theirs = {(e[\"task\"], e[\"Y\"], e[\"event_type\"].split(\"_\")[1]): e for e in jl(ROOT / \"results\" / \"c1_events.jsonl\")}\n    mism = []\n    for k, m in mine.items():\n        e = theirs.get(k)\n        if e is None:\n            mism.append(f\"{k}: missing in c1_events\")\n            continue\n        if not (abs(m[\"avoidance\"] - e[\"avoidance\"]) < TOL or (math.isnan(m[\"avoidance\"]) and e[\"avoidance\"] is None)):\n            mism.append(f\"{k}: avoidance {m['avoidance']} vs {e['avoidance']}\")\n        if m[\"n_nonY\"] != e[\"n_nonY\"] or m[\"gate\"] != e[\"gate_pass\"] or m[\"dis\"] != e[\"disagreeing\"]:\n            mism.append(f\"{k}: n_nonY/gate/disagreeing differ\")\n        if m[\"landing\"] != e[\"landing_counts\"]:\n            mism.append(f\"{k}: landing differs\")\n        for f in FAM:\n            if abs(m[\"ls\"][f] - e[\"logscore\"][f]) > 1e-9 or m[\"t1\"][f] != sorted(e[\"top1\"][f]):\n                mism.append(f\"{k}: {f} logscore/top1 differ ({m['ls'][f]} vs {e['logscore'][f]})\")\n    if len(theirs) != len(mine):\n        mism.append(f\"event count {len(mine)} vs {len(theirs)}\")\n    summ = jread(ROOT / \"results\" / \"c1_summary.json\")\n    head = {}\n    for arm in (\"named\", \"unnamed\"):\n        sel = [m for k, m in mine.items() if k[2] == arm and m[\"gate\"] and m[\"dis\"]]\n        w = wins_of(sel)\n        adv = [f for f in FAM if w[f] >= 3]\n        tests = []\n        if len(sel) >= 10:\n            ps = [exact_wilcoxon_p([e[\"ls\"][a] - e[\"ls\"][b] for e in sel]) for a, b in PAIRS]\n            tests = [{\"pair\": f\"{a}-{b}\", \"p\": p, \"p_holm\": q,\n                      \"mean_diff\": sum(e[\"ls\"][a] - e[\"ls\"][b] for e in sel) / len(sel)}\n                     for (a, b), p, q in zip(PAIRS, ps, holm(ps))]\n        head[arm] = {\"n_gated\": sum(1 for k, m in mine.items() if k[2] == arm and m[\"gate\"]),\n                     \"n_gated_disagreeing\": len(sel), \"wins\": w, \"advancing\": adv, \"tests\": tests}\n        ref = summ[f\"c1_{arm}\"]\n        if ref[\"n_gated\"] != head[arm][\"n_gated\"] or ref[\"n_gated_disagreeing\"] != len(sel):\n            mism.append(f\"{arm}: gated counts differ\")\n        if ref[\"rule\"][\"wins\"] != w or sorted(ref[\"rule\"][\"advancing\"]) != sorted(adv):\n            mism.append(f\"{arm}: wins/advancing differ {ref['rule']['wins']} vs {w}\")\n        if tests and ref[\"tests\"].get(\"run\"):\n            # This exact test enumerates the actual (tied) half-ranks; scipy's exact mode assumes untied ranks and its\n            # normal approximation has its own tie correction, so p-values may differ slightly.  A mismatch is a\n            # different Holm decision at 0.05 or a p gap > 0.01; every gap is reported.\n            gaps = []\n            for t_mine, t_ref in zip(tests, ref[\"tests\"][\"tests\"]):\n                gaps.append({\"pair\": t_mine[\"pair\"], \"p_audit\": t_mine[\"p\"], \"p_frozen\": t_ref[\"p\"],\n                             \"p_holm_audit\": t_mine[\"p_holm\"], \"p_holm_frozen\": t_ref[\"p_holm\"],\n                             \"method_frozen\": t_ref.get(\"method\"), \"n_nonzero\": t_ref.get(\"n_nonzero\")})\n                if (t_mine[\"p_holm\"] < 0.05) != (t_ref[\"p_holm\"] < 0.05) or abs(t_mine[\"p\"] - t_ref[\"p\"]) > 0.01:\n                    mism.append(f\"{arm} {t_mine['pair']}: p {t_mine['p']:.6g} vs {t_ref['p']:.6g} (decision or gap)\")\n            head[arm][\"p_value_comparison\"] = gaps\n            head[arm][\"max_abs_p_gap\"] = max(abs(g[\"p_audit\"] - g[\"p_frozen\"]) for g in gaps)\n    # exploratory three-family contest (LADDER / ASSOCIATE / PROXIMITY), recomputed independently\n    fam3 = [\"LADDER\", \"ASSOCIATE\", \"PROXIMITY\"]\n    ref3 =       w3[f] += 1\n\n    def hit_of(m, f):\n        mod = argmax_set(m[\"landing\"])\n        return len(set(mod) & set(m[\"t1\"][f])) / (len(mod) * len(m[\"t1\"][f])) if mod and m[\"t1\"][f] else 0.0\n\n    hit3 = {f: (sum(hit_of(m, f) for m in g3) / len(g3)) if g3 else None for f in fam3}\n    head[\"three_family_named\"] = {\"n_events\": len(s3), \"wins\": {f: w3[f] for f in fam3}, att = [r[\"label\"] for r in rows.get((t, y, \"copy\"), []) if r[\"label\"] != \"invalid\"]\n        cr[key] = att.count(y) / len(att) if att else None\n    head[\"copy_rate_mean\"] = sum(v for v in head[\"named_avoidance_mean\"] = sum(nav) / len(nav) if nav else None\n    # snap-back to the default: mean share of attempts equal to the context-free default, per arm\n    dshare = {}\n    for arm ### [58] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 10:23:38 UTC

```
File: /tmp/claude-10001/-ai-inventor-aii-data-runs-run-jSassd-YWLVC-4-gen-paper-repo--4-gen-demo-art-notebook-workspaces-iter-3-art-WiY4wqHLb9Gy/01bd1f95-7b73-4b31-ba70-6ee08f081105/scratchpad/build_mini_demo_data.py

"""Build mini_demo_data.json for the exposure-ban demo notebook.

Source dataset: full_method_out.json -> datasets["exposure_events"] (all 66 events = 33 banned algorithms Y x
{NAMED, UNNAMED}).  Every event keeps its original exp_gen_sol_out fields; three fields the frozen scoring code
needs but the dataset row does not carry are added from the artifact's committed files:
  metadata_survivors                 results/c1_events.jsonl (catalogue minus Y, catalogue order)
  metadata_proximity_token_distance  predictions/proximity.json (token-distance PROXIMITY sensitivity forecast)
  metadata_veto_rounds               predictions/veto_rounds.json (per-round vetoed n-grams and re-emergence of Y)
Usage: python -I build_mini_demo_data.py <artifact_dir> <out_json>
"""

import json
import math
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def clean(o):
    """NaN / inf -> None so the file is strict JSON."""
    if isinstance(o, float) and (math.isnan(o) or math.isinf(o)):
        return None
    if isinstance(o, dict):
        return {k: clean(v) for k, v in o.items()}
    if isinstance(o, list):
        return [clean(v) for v in o]
    return o


def main() -> None:
    art, out = Path(sys.argv[1]), Path(sys.argv[2])
    full = json.loads((art / "full_method_out.json").read_text())
    ds = next(d for d in full["datasets"] if d["dataset"] == "exposure_events")
    c1e = [json.loads(x) for x in (art / "results" / "c1_events.jsonl").read_text().splitlines() if x.strip()]
    prox = json.loads((art / "predictions" / "proximity.json").read_text())
    vr = json.loads((art / "predictions" / "veto_rounds.json").read_text())
    elig = json.loads((art / "predictions" / "eligibility.json").read_text())
    summ = json.loads((art / "results" / "c1_summary.json").read_text())
    red = json.loads((art / "results" / "rederive.json").read_text())
    rep = json.loads((art / "results" / "other_reports.json").read_text())
    smoke = json.loads((art / "results" / "smoke_tests.json").read_text())
    prompts = {}
    for line in (art / "predictions" / "prompts.jsonl").read_text().splitlines():
        if line.strip():
            r = json.loads(line)
            if r["prompt_key"] in ("T03:size_k_heap:copy:p0", "T03:size_k_heap:named:p0", "T03:size_k_heap:unnamed:p0"):
                prompts[r["arm"]] = {"prompt_key": r["prompt_key"], "user_text": r["user_text"],
                                     "chat_sha256": r["chat_sha256"], "n_prompt_tokens": r["n_prompt_tokens"]}
    ngr = json.loads((art / "predictions" / "ngrams.json").read_text())

    examples = []
    assert len(ds["examples"]) == len(c1e) == 66
    for ex, ce in zip(ds["examples"], c1e):
        inp = json.loads(ex["input"])
        assert (inp["task"], inp["banned_Y"], ex["metadata_event_type"]) == (ce["task"], ce["Y"], ce["event_type"])
        key = f"{inp['task']}:{inp['banned_Y']}"
        e = dict(ex)
        e["metadata_survivors"] = ce["survivors"]
        e["metadata_proximity_token_distance"] = prox[key]["distribution_token_distance"]
        e["metadata_veto_rounds"] = {
            "round_used": vr[key]["round_used"],
            "rounds": {r: {k: v[k] for k in ("mask_text", "n_programs", "n_invalid", "n_attempts", "n_Y", "reemerge",
                                             "n_nonY", "landing", "pass_rate", "default_share",
                                             "vetoed_ngram_occurrences")}
                       for r, v in vr[key]["rounds"].items()}}
        examples.append(clean(e))

    def keep_c1(r):
        return {"n_events_total": r["n_events_total"], "n_gated": r["n_gated"],
                "n_gated_disagreeing": r["n_gated_disagreeing"], "wins": r["rule"]["wins"],
                "advancing": r["rule"]["advancing"], "branch": r["branch"], "unaimed_share": r["unaimed_share"],
                "tests": [{k: t[k] for k in ("pair", "mean_diff", "ci95_task_cluster", "p", "p_holm", "method",
                                             "n_nonzero")} for t in r["tests"]["tests"]]}

    dsa = rep["default_share_by_arm"]
    default_share_mean = {}
    for arm in ("copy", "veto_final", "named", "unnamed"):
        v = [x[arm] for x in dsa.values() if arm in x and x[arm] is not None and x[arm] == x[arm]]
        default_share_mean[arm] = sum(v) / len(v)
    reference = {
        "_note": "Numbers reported by the original full run (results/c1_summary.json, results/rederive.json, "
                 "results/other_reports.json).  The event-level ones are recomputed by the notebook; the sample-level "
                 "ones need the 10,600 generated programs and are shown for context only.",
        "c1_named": keep_c1(summ["c1_named"]),
        "c1_unnamed": keep_c1(summ["c1_unnamed"]),
        "hit_rate_gated_named": summ["hit_rate_gated_named"],
        "mean_logscore_gated_disagreeing_named": summ["mean_logscore_gated_disagreeing_named"],
        "sensitivities": {k: {"wins": v["wins"], "branch": v["branch"], "n_gated_disagreeing": v["n_gated_disagreeing"]}
                          for k, v in summ["sensitivities"].items() if isinstance(v, dict) and "wins" in v},
        "placebos": summ["placebos"],
        "three_family_named": {k: v for k, v in summ["exploratory_not_preregistered"]["three_family_named"].items()
                               if k != "tests"},
        "audit_placebo": red["placebo"],
        "audit_n_mismatches": red["n_mismatches"],
        "advance_rule_null_calibration": summ["advance_rule_null_calibration"],
        "sample_level_context": {
            "n_samples": summ["n_samples"], "n_by_arm": summ["n_by_arm"],
            "labeller_sources": summ["labeller_sources"],
            "copy_rate_summary": rep["copy_rate_summary"],
            "default_share_mean_by_arm": default_share_mean,
            "named_vs_unnamed_summary": rep["named_vs_unnamed_summary"],
            "c1_exposure": {k: summ["c1_exposure"][k] for k in ("n_events", "verdict")},
            "gpu_hours_completed_runs": summ["gpu_hours"],
            "openrouter_spend_usd": summ["openrouter_spend_usd"],
            "smoke_tests": {"key": smoke["key"], "g_star_text": smoke["g_star_text"],
                            "veto_occurrences": smoke["veto_occurrences"], "copy_occurrences": smoke["copy_occurrences"],
                            "inert_identical_to_plain": smoke["inert_veto"]["identical_to_plain"],
                            "twin_consistent": smoke["twin_diagnostic"]["n_consistent"],
                            "twin_n": smoke["twin_diagnostic"]["n"], "passed": smoke["passed"]},
        },
    }
    key0 = "T03:size_k_heap"
    meta = {
        "title": "Show a solution, ban it, see what replaces it (exposure-ban experiment, Qwen3-8B bf16) - demo subset",
        "source": {"file": "full_method_out.json", "dataset": "exposure_events", "n_examples": len(examples),
                   "selection": "all 66 events (33 banned algorithms Y x NAMED / UNNAMED): the frozen C1 analysis "
                                "(advance rule, paired Wilcoxon tests, placebos) is defined over the whole event set"},
        "added_fields": {
            "metadata_survivors": "catalogue modes of the task minus Y (results/c1_events.jsonl)",
            "metadata_proximity_token_distance": "token-distance PROXIMITY forecast (predictions/proximity.json)",
            "metadata_veto_rounds": "iterated signature veto under the COPY prompt: vetoed n-grams (decoded) and "
                                    "re-emergence of Y per round (predictions/veto_rounds.json)"},
        "model": "Qwen/Qwen3-8B", "model_revision": "b968826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218",
        "task_order": ["T03", "T04", "T06", "T05", "T10", "R1", "T08", "R3", "T13", "R6", "T14", "T11"],
        "task_catalogue": {t: {"default": v["default"], "modes": v["modes"], "display_names": v["display_names"]}
                           for t, v in elig["tasks"].items()},
        "example_prompts": {"key": key0, "g_star_round1_text": ngr[key0]["decoded"], "g_star_round1_ids": ngr[key0]["ids"],
                            "arms": prompts},
        "reference_results": reference,
    }
    out.write_text(json.dumps(clean({"metadata": meta, "datasets": [{"dataset": "exposure_events",
                                                                       "examples": examples}]}), indent=1))
    print(f"wrote {out} ({out.stat().st_size / 1024:.1f} KB): {len(examples)} events")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

{
 "cells": [
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "id": "title",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": [
    "# Show a solution, ban it, see what replaces it\n",
    "\n",
    "**Exposure-ban experiment (Step 1b) on Qwen3-8B** (bf16, thinking off, vLLM 0.11.0, revision `b968826d`) over the 12 frozen tasks of the exp11 algorithm-choice bench.\n",
    "\n",
    "**The question.** A coding model is shown a correct program that uses algorithm **Y**, and is then told not to use Y. What does it write instead?\n",
    "\n",
    "Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\n",
    "\n",
    "| family | forecast of the replacement |\n",
    "|---|---|\n",
    "| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\n",
    "| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \"what is the standard alternative to Y?\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\n",
    "| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\n",
    "| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\n",
    "\n",
    "**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\n",
    "- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\n",
    "- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\n",
    "- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\n",
    "\n",
    "There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\n",
    "\n",
    "**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\n",
    "- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\n",
    "- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\n",
    "\n",
    "Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\n",
    "\n",
    "| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\n",
    "|---|---|\n",
    "| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\n",
    "| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\n",
    "| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\n",
    "| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\n",
    "| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\n",
    "| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\n",
    "| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "metadata": {
  "kernelspec": {
   "display_name": "Python 3",
   "language": "python",
   "name": "python3"
  },
  "language_info": {
   "name": "python",
   "version": "3.12"
  }
 },
 "nbformat": 4,
 "nbformat_minor": 5
}

What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\\n\",\n    \"| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |\"\n   ]\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\",\n   \"version\": \"3.12\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}\n", "updated_file": "{\n \"cells\": [\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"title\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"source\": [\n    \"# Show a solution, ban it, see what replaces it\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**Exposure-ban experiment (Step 1b) on Qwen3-8B** (bf16, thinking off, vLLM 0.11.0, revision `b968826d`) over the 12 frozen tasks of the exp11 algorithm-choice bench.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The question.** A coding model is shown a correct program that uses algorithm **Y**, and is then told not to use Y. What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\\n\",\n    \"| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |\"\n   ]\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"9ac80b3b\",\n   \"source\": \"import subprocess, sys\\ndef _pip(*a): subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', '-q', *a])\\n\\n# loguru — NOT on Colab, always install (method.py logs through versions)\\nif 'google.colab' not in sys.modules:\\n    _pip('numpy==2.0.2', 'scipy==1.16.3', 'matplotlib==3.10.0', 'pandas==2.2.2')\\n    _pip('torch==2.9.0', '--index-url', 'https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu')\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"execution_count\": null,\n   \"outputs\": []\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": \"f9dd0a10\",\n   \"source\": What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. \"title\",\n   \"metadata\": {},\n   \"source\": [\n    \"# Show a solution, ban it, see what replaces it\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**Exposure-ban experiment (Step 1b) on Qwen3-8B** (bf16, thinking off, vLLM 0.11.0, revision `b968826d`) over the 12 frozen tasks of the exp11 algorithm-choice bench.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The question.** A coding model is shown a correct program that uses algorithm **Y**, and is then told not to use Y. What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\\n\",\n    \"| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |\"\n   ]\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"4a0581dd\",\n   \"source\": \"## Setup: dependencies, imports and data\\n\\nThe install cell follows the Colab pattern. On Colab the pre-installed scientific stack is used as is. Locally the same versions are installed, plus `loguru`, which `method.py` uses for logging. `torch` is only needed for the n-gram-veto logits processor of section 4.\",\n   \"metadata\": {}\n  },\n What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\\n\",\n    \"| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |\"\n   ]\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"4a0581dd\",\n   \"source\": \"## Setup: dependencies, imports and data\\n\\nThe install cell follows the Colab pattern. On Colab the pre-installed scientific stack is used as is. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. They read the exp11 bench or the Qwen3-8B tokenizer, and nothing in this demo calls them.'}

What does it write instead?\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Four forecasts of the replacement were fixed and sha256 hash-chained *before* any ban sample was drawn:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| family | forecast of the replacement |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| **LADDER** | the model's context-free default algorithm for the task |\\n\",\n    \"| **ASSOCIATE** | the model's own modal answer to \\\"what is the standard alternative to Y?\\\" (asked and frozen earlier) |\\n\",\n    \"| **PROXIMITY** | the surviving algorithm whose reference programs are nearest the shown program (Zhang-Shasha tree-edit distance on identifier-normalised ASTs) |\\n\",\n    \"| **SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT** | where the model lands when Y's distinctive token n-gram is vetoed during decoding, under the same prompt with *no* ban text (iterated, persistent signature veto) |\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**The prompts.** There are 33 eligible non-default algorithms Y. Nineteen of them are *priority* Y, whose associate differs from the default. For each Y, the prompt shows up to 5 name-scrubbed correct programs of Y, then asks one of three things:\\n\",\n    \"- **COPY** (the matched twin): `Write a correct solution.`\\n\",\n    \"- **NAMED**: `Write a correct solution that does not use {Y}.`\\n\",\n    \"- **UNNAMED**: `Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.`\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"There are N = 50 samples per Y and arm. The seed of each (task, Y, shown program, sample index) is shared by every arm, so the arms use common random numbers.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"**What this notebook runs.** `method.py` is a CLI with 8 subcommands. Four of them cannot run in a 10-minute CPU demo:\\n\",\n    \"- `setup` and `forecasts` need the exp11 bench.\\n\",\n    \"- `stage1` and `stage2` need one GPU running Qwen3-8B on vLLM for about 35 GPU-minutes. They generate and label 10,600 programs.\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"Their outputs are condensed into `mini_demo_data.json`: all **66 events** (33 Y × NAMED / UNNAMED). Each event carries its landing counts, its gate statistics and the four committed forecasts. The notebook re-runs every CPU step on them, with the original code:\\n\",\n    \"\\n\",\n    \"| `method.py` subcommand | in this notebook |\\n\",\n    \"|---|---|\\n\",\n    \"| `setup`, `forecasts` (bench) | shown as code; their outputs (defaults, associates, PROXIMITY forecasts, allocation, prompts) are rebuilt from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit1` | **runs**: the forecasts and the frozen analysis code are sha256 hash-chained (COMMIT #1) |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage1` (GPU) | **runs on CPU**: the original n-gram-veto logits processor drives a toy decoder through the smoke tests, and SUBTRACT-IN-CONTEXT is recomputed from the stored veto rounds |\\n\",\n    \"| `commit2` | **runs**: COMMIT #2 and chain verification |\\n\",\n    \"| `stage2` (GPU) | the NAMED / UNNAMED outcomes are read from the data |\\n\",\n    \"| `analyze` | **runs**: the frozen C1 analysis (scoring, advance rule, Holm-corrected paired Wilcoxon tests, task-cluster bootstrap CIs, branch, sensitivities, placebos) |\\n\",\n    \"| `audit` | **runs**: the independent re-derivation `audit/rederive.py`, with its placebos |\"\n   ]\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"4a0581dd\",\n   \"source\": \"## Setup: dependencies, imports and data\\n\\nThe install cell follows the Colab pattern. 1024\nJUDGE_SOFT_CAP = 0.50  # stop new judge calls (USD); the hard cap of the plan is 0.60\n\nNL = "\\n"\nINTRO = "Here is a correct solution to this task:"\nWORDINGS = {\n    "copy": "Write a correct solution.",\n    "named": "Write a correct solution that does not use {x}.",  # = frozen template W5\n    "unnamed": "Write a correct solution that uses a different algorithm from the one above.",\n}\n# Frozen ban templates W1-W4 of the shared protocol (round-2 prompts_v2.json); W5 is the NAMED wording above.\nTEMPLATES_W1_W4 = {\n    "W1": "Do not use {x} for this task.",\n    "W2": "Your solution must not rely on {x}.",\n    "W3": "Avoid {x} entirely.",\n    "W4": "{x} is not allowed here.",\n}\n\n\ndef setup_logging(name: str) -> None:\n    LOGS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    logger.remove()\n    logger.add(sys.stdout, level="INFO", format="{time:HH:mm:ss}|{level:<7}|{message}")\n    logger.add(LOGS / f"{name}.log", rotation="30 MB", level="DEBUG")\n\n\ndef sha256_text(s: str) -> str:\n    return hashlib.sha256(s.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()\n\n\ndef sha256_file(p: Path) -> str:\n    h = hashlib.sha256()\n    with Path(p).open("rb") as f:\n        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(1 << 20), b""):\n            h.update(chunk)\n    return h.hexdigest()\n\n\ndef canon_json(obj) -> str:\n    return json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)\n\n\ndef seed_of(key: str) -> int:\n    """Seed shared by every arm of one (task, Y, shown program, sample index): common random numbers."""\n    return int(sha256_text(key)[:8], 16)\n\n\ndef read_jsonl(p: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    if not Path(p).exists():\n        return []\n    out = []\n    with Path(p).open() as f:\n        for line in f:\n            line = line.strip()\n            if line:\n                out.append(json.loads(line))\n    return out\n\n\n_write_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n\ndef append_jsonl(p: Path, rows: list[dict]) -> None:\n    with _write_lock, Path(p).open("a") as f:\n        for r in rows:\n            f.write(json.dumps(r) + "\\n")\n        f.flush()\n\n\ndef write_json(p: Path, obj, indent: int = 1) -> None:\n    Path(p).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    Path(p).write_text(json.dumps(obj, indent=indent, default=_json_default))\n\n\ndef _json_default(o):\n    if hasattr(o, "item"):\n        return o.item()\n    if isinstance(o, (set, tuple)):\n        return list(o)\n    raise TypeError(f"not JSON serialisable: {type(o)}")\n\n\ndef read_json(p: Path):\n    return json.loads(Path(p).read_text())\n\n\ndef fenced(code: str) -> str:\n    """The exact fenced text used for n-gram tokenisation and for the shown-program prompt block."""\n    return "```python" + NL + code.rstrip() + NL + "```"\n\n\ndef wilson(k: int, n: int, z: float = 1.959964) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    if n == 0:\n        return (float("nan"), float("nan"))\n    p = k / n\n    d = 1 + z * z / n\n    c = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / d\n    h = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z * z / (4 * n * n)) / d\n    return (max(0.0, c - h), min(1.0, c + h))\n\n\nclass Timer:\n    """Accumulates wall and GPU seconds per stage into results/timings.json."""\n\n    path = RESULTS / "timings.json"\n\n    def __init__(self, stage: str, gpu: bool = False):\n        self.stage, self.gpu = stage, gpu\n\n    def __enter__(self):\n        self.t0 = time.time()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, *exc):\n        dt = time.time() - self.t0\n        RESULTS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n        tm = read_json(self.path) if self.path.exists() else {}\n        e = tm.setdefault(self.stage, {"wall_s": 0.0, "gpu_s": 0.0, "runs": 0})\n        e["wall_s"] += dt\n        e["gpu_s"] += dt if self.gpu else 0.0\n        e["runs"] += 1\n        e["last_end_utc"] = time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime())\n        write_json(self.path, tm)\n        logger.info(f"[timing] {self.stage}: {dt:.1f}s ({\'GPU\' if self.gpu else \'CPU\'})")\n        return False\n\n\ndef deviation(text: str) -> None:\n    """Record a declared deviation from the plan (results/deviations.jsonl), deduplicated by text."""\n    p = RESULTS / "deviations.jsonl"\n    RESULTS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n    have = {r["text"] for r in read_jsonl(p)}\n    if text not in have:\n        append_jsonl(p, [{"ts": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()), "text": text}])\n        logger.warning(f"DEVIATION: {text}")'}

{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_WiY4wqHLb9Gy/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'd807e381', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': '## 2. `setup` and `forecasts`: the ban-free forecasts (CPU, need the exp11 bench)\n\n**`setup`** does four things:\n- copies exp11\'s `bench/` and verifies its 158 committed sha256 hashes;\n- re-checks the gated labeller on 320 gold programs (it reproduces every `gate.json` value);\n- recounts the defaults and eligibility: 33 Y, 19 of them priority;\n- writes COMMIT #0, the hashes of the inputs the bench commitment does not list.\n\n**`forecasts`** builds the shown programs and three of the four forecasts:\n- **Shown programs.** Each pool goes through name scrubbing, hidden tests, relabelling, canonical-token deduplication and a cap of ≤ 600 tokens.\n- **The distinctive n-gram g\\*** of each Y, chosen by the frozen rule.\n- **The allocation** of the N = 50 samples over the shown programs.\n- **PROXIMITY**: the median Zhang-Shasha distance from each shown program to ≤ 20 reference programs of every survivor (2,972 pairs).\n- **LADDER** (the default) and **ASSOCIATE** (the frozen associate).\n- **The 775 rendered prompts.**\n\nBoth drivers need the bench, which holds the forced programs, hidden tests and labeller, and the forecasts also need the Qwen3-8B tokenizer. They are shown here as reference and are not called:\n\n```python\ndef cmd_setup(args) -> None:\n    from exposure.forecasts import copy_and_verify, defaults_and_eligibility, labeller_recheck\n    _cpu_ram_limit(40)\n    with Timer("setup"):\n        info = env_info()\n        logger.info(f"environment: {json.dumps(info)}")\n        write_json(RESULTS / "environment.json", info)\n        ver = copy_and_verify()\n        from bench_copy.common.commit import commit, verify_chain\n        if not COMMIT_LOG.exists() or not any(r["kind"] == "commit0_inputs" for r in read_jsonl(COMMIT_LOG)):\n            payload = {"bench_commitment_combined_sha256": ver["combined_sha256"],\n                       "uncommitted_inputs": ver["uncommitted_input_sha256"]}\n            write_json(PRED / "commit0_inputs.json", payload)\n            commit(COMMIT_LOG, "commit0_inputs", sha256_file(PRED / "commit0_inputs.json"),\n                   {"file": "predictions/commit0_inputs.json"})\n            assert verify_chain(COMMIT_LOG)\n        rc = labeller_recheck(workers=NUM_CPUS)\n        flagged = [t for t, v in rc.items() if v["flag"]]\n        if flagged:\n            deviation(f"labeller re-check flags tasks {flagged} (more than 5 points below gate.json or a reference "\n                      "mislabelled); their events are sampled but reported outside the primary")\n        elig = defaults_and_eligibility()\n        elig["labeller_flagged_tasks"] = flagged\n        write_json(PRED / "eligibility.json", elig)\n\n\ndef cmd_forecasts(args) -> None:\n    from exposure.core import load_tokenizer\n    from exposure.forecasts import allocation, ngrams_and_shown, proximity, render_prompts, shown_pools\n    _cpu_ram_limit(40)\n    with Timer("forecasts"):\n        tok = load_tokenizer()\n        elig = read_json(PRED / "eligibility.json")\n        pools = shown_pools(elig, tok, workers=NUM_CPUS)\n        write_json(RESULTS / "shown_pool_summary.json",\n                   {k: {"n_R": v["n_R"], "n_pool": len(v["kept"]), "drops": v["drops"]} for k, v in pools.items()})\n        ngrams, shown = ngrams_and_shown(elig, pools, tok)\n        alloc = {k: allocation(len(v)) for k, v in shown.items()}\n        prox = proximity(elig, shown, alloc, workers=NUM_CPUS)\n        e_by = {f"{e[\'task\']}:{e[\'Y\']}": e for e in elig["eligible"]}\n        ladder = {k: {e_by[k]["default"]: 1.0} for k in shown}\n        assoc = {k: e_by[k]["associate"] for k in shown}\n        cov = {k: {"coveto_groups": v["coveto_groups"], "coveto_modes": v["coveto_modes"],\n                   "default_coveto": v["default_coveto"], "associate_coveto": v["associate_coveto"],\n                   "n_ref_lt5": v["n_ref_lt5"]} for k, v in ngrams.items()}\n        prompts = render_prompts(tok, shown)\n        ...  # writes shown_programs.jsonl, ngrams.json, coveto.json, associate.json, proximity.json, ladder.json,\n             # allocation.json and prompts.jsonl into predictions/\n```\n\n**What the demo does instead.** Every event in `mini_demo_data.json` carries the outputs of these two steps for its Y. The next cell rebuilds the eligibility table and the LADDER / ASSOCIATE / PROXIMITY forecasts from them. It also re-runs the original `allocation()`, and writes the files under the same names that `cmd_forecasts` writes. That way COMMIT #1 can freeze them and `analyze` can read them back.'}

per task: default, catalogue modes, display names\nby_key = defaultdict(dict)                            # "task:Y" -> {"named": event, "unnamed": event}\nfor ex in events_raw:\n    inp = json.loads(ex["input"])\n    by_key[f"{inp[\'task\']}:{inp[\'banned_Y\']}"][ex["metadata_event_type"].split("_")[1]] = ex\nKEYS = list(by_key)[:N_Y]                             # frozen order: TASK_ORDER, then Y order within a task\nelig = {"tasks": CATALOGUE,\n        "eligible": [{"task": k.split(":")[0], "Y": k.split(":")[1],\n                      "default": by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_default"],\n                      "associate": by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_forecasts"]["ASSOCIATE"],\n                      "priority": by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_priority"]} for k in KEYS]}\nn_shown = {k: by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_n_pY"] for k in KEYS}\n\n\n# exposure/forecasts.py: split the N = 50 samples of an arm as evenly as possible over the k shown programs\ndef allocation(k: int, n: int = N_PER_ARM) -> list[int]:\n    base, rem = divmod(n, k)\n    return [base + (1 if i < rem else 0) for i in range(k)]\n\n\n# --- as in cmd_forecasts ---\ne_by = {f"{e[\'task\']}:{e[\'Y\']}": e for e in elig["eligible"]}\nalloc = {k: allocation(n_shown[k]) for k in KEYS}\nladder = {k: {e_by[k]["default"]: 1.0} for k in KEYS}\nassoc = {k: e_by[k]["associate"] for k in KEYS}\nprox = {k: {"distribution": by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_forecasts"]["PROXIMITY"],\n            "distribution_token_distance": by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_proximity_token_distance"]} for k in KEYS}\nwrite_json(PRED / "eligibility.json", elig)\nwrite_json(PRED / "associate.json", assoc)\nwrite_json(PRED / "proximity.json", prox)\nwrite_json(PRED / "ladder.json", ladder)\nwrite_json(PRED / "allocation.json", alloc)\nlogger.info(f"forecasts written for {len(KEYS)} Y")\nassert all(by_key[k]["named"]["metadata_forecasts"]["LADDER"] == ladder[k] for k in KEYS)  # LADDER == {default: 1}\n\nfc_table = pd.DataFrame([{\n    "task:Y": k, "priority": e_by[k]["priority"], "shown programs": n_shown[k], "allocation of 50": alloc[k],\n    "LADDER (default)": e_by[k]["default"], "ASSOCIATE": "/".join(assoc[k]),\n    "PROXIMITY (ZSS)": ", ".join(f"{a} {b:.2f}" for a, b in prox[k]["distribution"].items()),\n    "PROXIMITY (token)": ", ".join(f"{a} {b:.2f}" for a, b in prox[k]["distribution_token_distance"].items()),\n    "seed of p0:s0": seed_of(f"{k}:p0:s0")} for k in KEYS])\ndisplay(fc_table)'}
